Most of Tennessee has a [humid subtropical climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humid_subtropical_climate), with the exception of some of the higher elevations in the Appalachians, which are classified as a cooler [mountain temperate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oceanic_climate) or [humid continental climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humid_continental_climate).[^cite_note-Climate-243] The [Gulf of Mexico](21076367.md) is the dominant factor in Tennessee's climate, with winds from the south responsible for most of the state's annual precipitation. Generally, the state has hot summers and mild to cool winters with generous precipitation throughout the year. The highest average monthly precipitation usually occurs between December and April. The driest months, on average, are August to October. The state receives an average of 50 inches (130 cm) of precipitation annually. Snowfall ranges from 5 inches (13 cm) in West Tennessee to over 80 inches (200 cm) in East Tennessee's highest mountains.[^cite_note-Tennessee_Agriculture-244][^cite_note-245]

Summers are generally hot and humid, with most of the state averaging a high of around 90 °F (32 °C). Winters tend to be mild to cool, decreasing in temperature at higher elevations. For areas outside the highest mountains, the average overnight lows are generally near freezing. The highest recorded temperature was 113 °F (45 °C) at [Perryville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Perryville,_Tennessee) on August 9, 1930, while the lowest recorded temperature was −32 °F (−36 °C) at [Mountain City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mountain_City,_Tennessee) on December 30, 1917.[^cite_note-246]

While Tennessee is far enough from the coast to avoid any direct impact from a hurricane, its location makes it susceptible to the remnants of tropical [cyclones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyclone), which weaken over land and can cause significant rainfall.[^cite_note-WPCSE-247] The state annually averages about 50 days of thunderstorms, which can be severe with large hail and damaging winds.[^cite_note-Thunderstorm_Hazard-248] Tornadoes are possible throughout the state, with West and Middle Tennessee the most vulnerable. The state averages 15 tornadoes annually.[^cite_note-Annual_Average_Number_of_Tornadoes-249] They can be severe, and the state leads the nation in the percentage of total tornadoes that have fatalities.[^cite_note-Tornado_Top_10_Lists-250] Winter storms such as in [1993](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1993_Storm_of_the_Century) and [2021](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/February_13%E2%80%9317,_2021_North_American_winter_storm) occur occasionally, and [ice storms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ice_storm) are fairly common. Fog is a persistent problem in some areas, especially in East Tennessee.[^cite_note-251]

## Cities, towns, and counties

See also: [List of municipalities in Tennessee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_municipalities_in_Tennessee) and [List of counties in Tennessee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_counties_in_Tennessee)

Tennessee is divided into [95 counties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_counties_in_Tennessee), each of which has a [county seat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/County_seat).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETennessee_Blue_Book_2005-2006616–617-253] The state has [340 municipalities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_municipalities_in_Tennessee) in total.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETennessee_Blue_Book_2005-2006618–625-254] The [Office of Management and Budget](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Office_of_Management_and_Budget) designates [ten metropolitan areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_metropolitan_areas_of_Tennessee) in Tennessee, four of which extend into neighboring states.[^cite_note-255]

Nashville is Tennessee's capital and largest city, with nearly 700,000 residents.[^cite_note-PopEstCities-256] Its [13-county metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nashville_metropolitan_area) has been the state's largest since the early 1990s and is one of the nation's fastest-growing metropolitan areas, with about 2 million residents.[^cite_note-257] Memphis, with more than 630,000 inhabitants, was the state's largest city until 2016, when Nashville surpassed it.[^cite_note-commercialappeal0517-2] It is in [Shelby County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shelby_County,_Tennessee), Tennessee's largest county in both population and land area.[^cite_note-258] [Knoxville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Knoxville,_Tennessee), with about 190,000 inhabitants, and [Chattanooga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chattanooga,_Tennessee), with about 180,000 residents, are the third- and fourth-largest cities, respectively.[^cite_note-PopEstCities-256] [Clarksville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clarksville,_Tennessee) is a significant population center, with about 170,000 residents.[^cite_note-PopEstCities-256] [Murfreesboro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Murfreesboro,_Tennessee) is the sixth-largest city and Nashville's largest [suburb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suburb), with more than 150,000 residents.[^cite_note-PopEstCities-256] In addition to the major cities, the [Tri-Cities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tri-Cities,_Tennessee) of [Kingsport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingsport,_Tennessee), [Bristol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bristol,_Tennessee), and [Johnson City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Johnson_City,_Tennessee) are considered the sixth major population center.[^cite_note-259]

The [2020 United States census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_United_States_census) reported Tennessee's population at 6,910,840, an increase of 564,735, or 8.90%, since the [2010 census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_United_States_census).[^cite_note-PopEstUS-4] Between 2010 and 2019, the state received a natural increase of 143,253 (744,274 births minus 601,021 deaths), and an increase from net migration of 338,428 people into the state. [Immigration](15051.md) from outside the U.S. resulted in a net increase of 79,086, and migration within the country produced a net increase of 259,342.[^cite_note-PopCompUS-261] Tennessee's [center of population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Center_of_population) is in [Murfreesboro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Murfreesboro,_Tennessee) in [Rutherford County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rutherford_County,_Tennessee).[^cite_note-262]

According to the 2020 census, 5.7% of Tennessee's population were under age 5, 22.1% were under 18, and 17.1% were 65 or older.[^cite_note-2020DP1-263] In recent years, Tennessee has been a top destination for domestic migration, receiving an influx of people relocating from places such as [California](5407.md), the [Northeast](431669.md), and the [Midwest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midwestern_United_States) due to the low cost of living and booming employment opportunities.[^cite_note-264][^cite_note-265] In 2019, about 5.5% of Tennessee's population was foreign-born. Of the foreign-born population, approximately 42.7% were naturalized citizens and 57.3% non-citizens.[^cite_note-stateprofile-266] The foreign-born population consisted of approximately 49.9% from [Latin America](18524.md), 27.1% from [Asia](689.md), 11.9% from [Europe](9239.md), 7.7% from [Africa](5334607.md), 2.7% from [Northern America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_America), and 0.6% from [Oceania](22621.md).[^cite_note-267] In 2018, The top countries of origin for Tennessee's immigrants were [Mexico](3966054.md), [India](14533.md), [Honduras](13394.md), [China](5405.md) and [Egypt](8087628.md).[^cite_note-268]

With the exception of a slump in the 1980s, Tennessee has been one of the fastest-growing states in the nation since 1970, benefiting from the larger [Sun Belt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sun_Belt) phenomenon.[^cite_note-269] The state has been a top destination for people relocating from [Northeastern](431669.md) and [Midwestern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midwestern_United_States) states. This time period has seen the birth of new economic sectors in the state and has positioned the Nashville and Clarksville metropolitan areas as two of the fastest-growing regions in the country.[^cite_note-growthtacir-270] According to [HUD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Department_of_Housing_and_Urban_Development)'s 2022 [Annual Homeless Assessment Report](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annual_Homeless_Assessment_Report_to_Congress), there were an estimated 10,567 [homeless](19394651.md) people in Tennessee.[^cite_note-271][^cite_note-272]

Largest alone or in any combination ethnic origin by county in Tennessee, per the 2020 census

[^cite_note-286]

In 2020, 6.9% of the total population was of Hispanic or Latino origin (of any race), up from 4.6% in 2010. Between 2000 and 2010, Tennessee's Hispanic population grew by 134.2%, the third-highest rate of any state.[^cite_note-287] In 2020, [Non-Hispanic or Latino Whites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-Hispanic_or_Latino_Whites) were 70.9% of the population, compared to 57.7% of the population nationwide.[^cite_note-288] In 2010, the five most common self-reported ethnic groups in the state were American (26.5%), [English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_ancestry) (8.2%), [Irish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irish_ancestry) (6.6%), [German](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_ancestry) (5.5%), and [Scotch-Irish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scotch-Irish_Americans) (2.7%).[^cite_note-289] Most Tennesseans who self-identify as having [American ancestry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_ancestry) are of [English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_American) and [Scotch-Irish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scotch-Irish_American) ancestry. An estimated 21–24% of Tennesseans are of predominantly English ancestry.[^cite_note-Fischer1989-290][^cite_note-291] The top languages spoken in Tennessee after English are Spanish, Arabic, Chinese, Vietnamese, Korean, French, Laotian, Amharic, German, Gujarati, Japanese, Tagalog, Hindi, Russian, and Persian.[^cite_note-292]

### Religion

|  |  |  |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [Evangelical Protestantism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evangelicalism) |  | 45% |
| [Unaffiliated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irreligion) |  | 24% |
| [Mainline Protestantism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mainline_Protestant) |  | 12% |
| [Catholic](606848.md) |  | 7% |
| [Historically Black Protestantism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_church) |  | 6% |
| Other faiths |  | 3% |
| Other [Christianity](5211.md) |  | 1% |
| [Islam](6037917.md) |  | 1% |
| [Judaism](15624.md) |  | 1% |

Since its settlement, Tennessee has always been predominantly [Christian](5211.md). About 72% of the population identifies as Christian, with [Protestants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestant) making up 63% of the population. Of the Protestants in the state, [Evangelical Protestants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evangelicalism) compose 45% of the population, [Mainline Protestants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mainline_Protestant) 12%, and [Historically Black Protestants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_church) 6%. [Roman Catholics](410868.md) make up 7%, [Mormons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mormon) 1%, and [Orthodox Christians](10186.md) 1%.[^cite_note-auto-293] The largest denominations by number of adherents are the [Southern Baptist Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Baptist_Convention), the [United Methodist Church](32304.md), the Roman Catholic Church, and the [Churches of Christ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Churches_of_Christ).\[^cite_note-[www.thearda.com-294](http://www.thearda.com-294/)] [Muslims](6037917.md) make up about 1%, [Jews](15624.md) are slightly under 1%, and adherents of other religions make up about 3% of the population. About 24% of Tennesseans are [non-religious](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irreligion), with 15% identifying as "Nothing in particular", 5% as [agnostics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agnosticism), and 4% as [atheists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atheism).[^cite_note-auto-293]

Tennessee is included in most definitions of the [Bible Belt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bible_Belt), and is ranked as [one of the nation's most religious states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._states_and_territories_by_religiosity) across multiple metrics.[^cite_note-295] Several Protestant denominations have their headquarters in Tennessee, including the [Southern Baptist Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Baptist_Convention) and [National Baptist Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Baptist_Convention,_USA,_Inc.) (in Nashville); the [Church of God in Christ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_God_in_Christ) and the [Cumberland Presbyterian Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cumberland_Presbyterian_Church) (in Memphis);[^cite_note-296] and the [Church of God](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_God_\(Cleveland,_Tennessee\)) and the [Church of God of Prophecy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_God_of_Prophecy) (in [Cleveland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cleveland,_Tennessee));[^cite_note-297][^cite_note-298] and the [National Association of Free Will Baptists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Association_of_Free_Will_Baptists) (in [Antioch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antioch,_Tennessee)).[^cite_note-299] Nashville has publishing houses of several denominations.[^cite_note-300] Despite the state's religiousity, religious affiliation has declined, along with most of the United States; this is part of a larger trend of [Decline of Christianity in the Western World](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Decline_of_Christianity_in_the_Western_World). Between 2014 and 2024, the religiously unaffiliated increased ten percentage points according to [Pew Research Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pew_Research_Center), while Christians dropped nine points.[^cite_note-auto-293]

## Economy

Main article: [Economy of Tennessee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Tennessee)

See also: [List of Tennessee locations by per capita income](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Tennessee_locations_by_per_capita_income)

refer to caption

A geomap showing the counties of Tennessee colored by the relative range of that county's median income.

refer to caption

Chart showing poverty in Tennessee, by age and gender (red = female)

In 2025, Tennessee had a [gross state product](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gross_regional_domestic_product) of \$589.8 billion.[^cite_note-U.S._Bureau_of_Economic_Analysis-301] In 2025, the state's [per capita personal income](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Per_capita_income) was \$69,131.[^cite_note-U.S._Bureau_of_Economic_Analysis-301] The [median household income](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Household_income_in_the_United_States) was \$54,833.[^cite_note-stateprofile-266] About 13.6% percent of the population was below the [poverty line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poverty_line).[^cite_note-PopEstUS-4] In 2019, the state reported a total employment of 2,724,545 and a total number of 139,760 employer establishments.[^cite_note-PopEstUS-4] Tennessee is a [right-to-work](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Right-to-work_law) state, like most of its Southern neighbors.[^cite_note-302] [Unionization](17626.md) has historically been low and continues to decline, as in most of the U.S.[^cite_note-303] As of May 2025, the [unemployment rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unemployment_rate) was 3.5 percent.[^cite_note-304]

### Taxation

See also: [State income tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_income_tax)

Tennessee has a reputation as a low-tax state and is usually ranked as one of the five states with the lowest tax burden on residents.[^cite_note-305] Despite being low-tax, it is ranked third among U.S. states for fiscal health.[^cite_note-306] It is one of nine states that do not have a [general income tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_income_tax); the [sales tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sales_tax) is the primary means of funding the government.[^cite_note-307] The [Hall income tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hall_income_tax) was imposed on most [dividends](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dividend) and [interest](146738.md) at a rate of 6% but was completely phased out by 2021.[^cite_note-308] The first \$1,250 of individual income and \$2,500 of joint income was exempt from this tax.[^cite_note-309] [Property taxes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Property_tax) are the primary source of revenue for local governments.[^cite_note-tacir2002-310]

The state's sales and [use tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Use_tax) rate for most items is 7%, the second-highest in the nation, along with Mississippi, Rhode Island, New Jersey, and Indiana. Food is taxed at 4%, but candy, dietary supplements, and prepared foods are taxed at 7%.[^cite_note-311] Local sales taxes are collected in most jurisdictions at rates varying from 1.5% to 2.75%, bringing the total sales tax between 8.5% and 9.75%. The average combined rate is about 9.5%, the nation's highest average sales tax.[^cite_note-312] [Intangible property](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intangible_property) tax is assessed on the shares of stockholders of any loan, investment, insurance, or for-profit cemetery companies. The assessment ratio is 40% of the value times the jurisdiction's tax rate.[^cite_note-tacir2002-310] Since 2016, Tennessee has had no inheritance tax.[^cite_note-313]

### Agriculture

Tennessee has the [eighth-most farms in the nation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agriculture_in_Tennessee), which cover more than 40% of its land area and have an average size of about 155 acres (0.63 km<sup>2</sup>).[^cite_note-ff1-314] Cash receipts for crops and livestock have an estimated annual value of \$3.5 billion, and the agriculture sector has an estimated annual impact of \$81 billion on the state's economy.[^cite_note-ff1-314] [Beef cattle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beef_cattle) is the state's largest agricultural commodity, followed by [broilers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Broilers) and [poultry](23197.md).[^cite_note-homeandfarm13-19] Tennessee ranks 12th in the nation for the number of cattle, with more than half of its farmland dedicated to cattle grazing.[^cite_note-ff1-314][^cite_note-315] [Soybeans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soybean) and [corn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maize) are the state's first and second-most common crops, respectively,[^cite_note-homeandfarm13-19] and are most heavily grown in West and Middle Tennessee, especially the northwestern corner of the state.[^cite_note-316][^cite_note-317] Tennessee ranks seventh in the nation in [cotton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cotton) production, most of which is grown in the fertile soils of central West Tennessee.[^cite_note-nassreport-318]

The state ranks fourth nationwide in the production of [tobacco](30942.md), most significantly [burley tobacco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burley_tobacco), which is predominantly grown in the Ridge-and-Valley region of East Tennessee.[^cite_note-319][^cite_note-farmbureau-320] Tennessee farmers are also known worldwide for their cultivation of [tomatoes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tomato) and [horticultural](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Horticulture) plants.[^cite_note-321][^cite_note-322] Other important cash crops in the state include [hay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hay), [wheat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wheat), [eggs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egg_as_food), and [snap beans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Snap_bean).[^cite_note-ff1-314][^cite_note-farmbureau-320] The Nashville Basin is a top equestrian region, due to soils that produce grass favored by horses. The [Tennessee Walking Horse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee_Walking_Horse), first bred in the region in the late 18th century, is one of the world's most recognized horse breeds.[^cite_note-323] Tennessee also ranks second nationwide for [mule](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mule) breeding and the production of [goat meat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goat_meat).[^cite_note-farmbureau-320] The state's timber industry is largely concentrated on the Cumberland Plateau and ranks as a top producer of [hardwood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hardwood) nationwide.[^cite_note-324] [Tennessee whiskey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee_whiskey) is among the state's top ten exports; it undergoes [a distinctive manufacture and filtration process](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lincoln_County_Process), regulated under state law.[^cite_note-\:0-325]

### Industry

A Nissan Leaf, which is manufactured in Tennessee

A [Nissan Leaf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nissan_Leaf), one of six models manufactured at the [Nissan Smyrna Assembly Plant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nissan_Smyrna_Assembly_Plant), the largest automotive assembly plant in North America

Until [World War II](32927.md), Tennessee, like most Southern states, remained predominantly agrarian. Chattanooga became one of the first industrial cities in the south in the decades after the [Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Civil_War), when many factories, including iron foundries, steel mills, and textile mills were constructed there.[^cite_note-jsh-132] But most of Tennessee's industrial growth began with the federal investments in the [Tennessee Valley Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee_Valley_Authority) (TVA) and the [Manhattan Project](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manhattan_Project) in the 1930s and 1940s. The state's industrial and manufacturing sector continued to expand in the succeeding decades, and Tennessee is now home to more than 2,400 advanced manufacturing establishments, which produce a total of more than \$29 billion worth of goods annually.[^cite_note-326]

The [automotive industry](23576520.md) is Tennessee's largest manufacturing sector and one of the nation's largest.[^cite_note-commercialappealauto-327] [Nissan's assembly plant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nissan_Smyrna_Assembly_Plant) in [Smyrna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smyrna,_Tennessee) is the largest automotive assembly plant in North America.[^cite_note-328] Two other automakers have assembly plants in Tennessee: [General Motors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Motors) in [Spring Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spring_Hill_Manufacturing) and [Volkswagen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volkswagen) in [Chattanooga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volkswagen_Chattanooga_Assembly_Plant).[^cite_note-329] [Ford](30433662.md) is constructing [an assembly plant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blue_Oval_City) in [Stanton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stanton,_Tennessee) that is expected to be operational in 2029.[^cite_note-330] In addition, the state contains more than 900 automotive suppliers.[^cite_note-331] Nissan and [Mitsubishi Motors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mitsubishi_Motors) have their North American corporate headquarters in [Franklin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franklin,_Tennessee).[^cite_note-332][^cite_note-333] The state is also one of the top producers of [food and drink products](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Food_industry), its second-largest manufacturing sector.[^cite_note-nam-334] A number of well-known brands originated in Tennessee, and even more are produced there.[^cite_note-ustr-335] Tennessee also ranks as one of the largest producers of [chemicals](58721.md).[^cite_note-nam-334] Chemical products manufactured in Tennessee include [industrial chemicals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Industrial_chemicals), [paints](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paints), [pharmaceuticals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pharmaceuticals), [plastic resins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Synthetic_resin), and [soaps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soap) and [hygiene products](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Personal_care). Additional important products manufactured in Tennessee include [fabricated metal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metal_fabrication) products, [electrical equipment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electrical_equipment), [consumer electronics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Consumer_electronics) and [electrical appliances](185982.md), and nonelectrical machinery.[^cite_note-ustr-335]

### Business and technology

Aerial view of the Oak Ridge National Laboratory

Established in 1942, [Oak Ridge National Laboratory](38147.md) is the largest national laboratory in the Department of Energy system

[Tennessee's commercial sector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Tennessee#Business) is dominated by a wide variety of companies, but its largest service industries include health care, transportation, music and entertainment, banking, and finance. Large corporations with headquarters in Tennessee include [FedEx](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FedEx), [AutoZone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AutoZone), [International Paper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Paper), and [First Horizon Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Horizon_Corporation), all based in Memphis; [Pilot Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pilot_Company) and [Regal Entertainment Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regal_Entertainment_Group) in Knoxville; [Hospital Corporation of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hospital_Corporation_of_America) based in Nashville; [Unum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unum) in Chattanooga; Acadia Senior Living and [Community Health Systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Community_Health_Systems) in Franklin; Eastman Chemical headquartered in Kingsport; [Dollar General](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dollar_General) in Goodlettsville, and LifePoint Health, [Tractor Supply Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tractor_Supply_Company), and [Delek US](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delek_US) in Brentwood.[^cite_note-336][^cite_note-337] In 2025, 99.5% of businesses in Tennessee were [small businesses](276495.md), and employed 41.5% of the state's workforce.[^cite_note-338]

Since the 1990s, the geographical area between Oak Ridge and Knoxville has been known as the Tennessee Technology Corridor, with more than 500 high-tech firms in the region.[^cite_note-sherman-339] The [research and development](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Research_and_development) industry in Tennessee is also one of the largest employment sectors, mainly due to the prominence of [Oak Ridge National Laboratory](38147.md) (ORNL) and the [Y-12 National Security Complex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Y-12_National_Security_Complex) in the city of [Oak Ridge](151055.md). ORNL conducts scientific research in [materials science](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Materials_science), [nuclear physics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_physics), energy, [high-performance computing](37153.md), [systems biology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Systems_biology), and [national security](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_security_of_the_United_States), and is the largest [national laboratory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Department_of_Energy_national_laboratories) in the [Department of Energy](62866.md) (DOE) system by size.[^cite_note-bigproblems-159][^cite_note-340] In the 2020s, Tennessee became a hub for research and development of advanced nuclear power technology.[^cite_note-341] The technology sector is also a rapidly growing industry in Tennessee, particularly in the largest urban areas.[^cite_note-342]

### Energy and mineral production

Further information: [List of power stations in Tennessee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_power_stations_in_Tennessee) and [List of power stations operated by the Tennessee Valley Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_power_stations_operated_by_the_Tennessee_Valley_Authority)

Photograph of Norris Dam, a hydroelectric power station operated by the Tennessee Valley Authority (TVA)

[Norris Dam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norris_Dam), a [hydroelectric dam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hydroelectric_dam) operated by the [Tennessee Valley Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee_Valley_Authority).

Tennessee's electric utilities are regulated monopolies, as in many other states.[^cite_note-343][^cite_note-344] The [Tennessee Valley Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee_Valley_Authority) (TVA) owns over 90% of the state's generating capacity.[^cite_note-345] [Nuclear power](4131402.md) is Tennessee's largest source of electricity generation, producing about 43.4% of its power in 2021. The same year, 22.4% of the power was produced from [coal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coal_power_in_the_United_States), 17.8% from [natural gas](12438990.md), 15.8% from [hydroelectricity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hydroelectric_power_in_the_United_States), and 1.3% from other [renewables](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renewable_energy_in_the_United_States). About 59.7% of the electricity generated in Tennessee produces [no greenhouse gas emissions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Low-carbon_power).[^cite_note-346] Tennessee is home to the first [nuclear power reactor](22151.md) in the U.S. to begin operation in the 21st century, which is at the [Watts Bar Nuclear Plant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Watts_Bar_Nuclear_Plant) in [Rhea County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rhea_County,_Tennessee).[^cite_note-347] Tennessee was also an early leader in hydroelectric power,[^cite_note-348] and today is the third-largest hydroelectric power-producing state east of the [Rocky Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rocky_Mountains).[^cite_note-energyprofile-349] Tennessee is a net consumer of electricity, receiving power from other TVA facilities in neighboring states.[^cite_note-nyt-350]

Tennessee has very little petroleum and natural gas reserves, but is home to one oil refinery, in Memphis.[^cite_note-energyprofile-349] [Bituminous coal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bituminous_coal) is mined in small quantities in the Cumberland Plateau and Cumberland Mountains.[^cite_note-tdecmineral-351] There are sizable reserves of [lignite coal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lignite_coal) in West Tennessee that remain untapped.[^cite_note-tdecmineral-351] Coal production in Tennessee peaked in 1972, and today less than 0.1% of coal in the U.S. comes from Tennessee.[^cite_note-energyprofile-349] Tennessee is the nation's leading producer of [ball clay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ball_clay).[^cite_note-tdecmineral-351] Other major mineral products produced in Tennessee include [sand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sand), [gravel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gravel), [crushed stone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crushed_stone), [Portland cement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portland_cement), [marble](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marble), [sandstone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sandstone), [common clay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clay), [lime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lime_\(material\)), and [zinc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zinc).[^cite_note-tdecmineral-351][^cite_note-352] The [Copper Basin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Copper_Basin_\(Tennessee\)), in Tennessee's southeastern corner in Polk County, was one of the nation's most productive [copper mining](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Copper_mining_in_the_United_States) districts between the 1840s and 1980s, and supplied about 90% of the copper the Confederacy used during the Civil War.[^cite_note-353] Mining activities in the basin resulted in a major environmental disaster, which left the surrounding landscape barren for more than a century.[^cite_note-354] Iron ore was another major mineral mined in Tennessee until the early 20th century.[^cite_note-355] Tennessee was also a top producer of [phosphate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phosphate) until the early 1990s.[^cite_note-356]

### Tourism

Photograph of Gatlinburg with the Great Smoky Mountains in the background

The [resort city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Resort_city) of [Gatlinburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gatlinburg,_Tennessee) borders the [Great Smoky Mountains National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Smoky_Mountains_National_Park), which is the most visited national park in the United States.

[^cite_note-gsmnpnumbers-357]

Tennessee is the 11th-most visited state in the nation,[^cite_note-358] receiving a record of 146 million tourists in 2024.[^cite_note-359] Its top tourist attraction is the [Great Smoky Mountains National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Smoky_Mountains_National_Park), the most visited national park in the U.S., with more than 14 million visitors in 2021,[^cite_note-gsmnpnumbers-357] although numbers have slightly declined since then.[^cite_note-360] The park anchors a large tourism industry based primarily in nearby [Gatlinburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gatlinburg,_Tennessee) and [Pigeon Forge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pigeon_Forge,_Tennessee), which includes [Dollywood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dollywood), the most visited ticketed attraction in Tennessee.[^cite_note-traveltips-361] Attractions related to Tennessee's musical heritage are spread throughout the state.[^cite_note-362][^cite_note-363] Other top attractions include the [Tennessee State Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee_State_Museum) and [Parthenon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parthenon_\(Nashville\)) in Nashville; the [National Civil Rights Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Civil_Rights_Museum) and [Graceland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Graceland) in Memphis; [Lookout Mountain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lookout_Mountain), the [Chattanooga Choo-Choo Hotel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chattanooga_Choo-Choo_Hotel), [Ruby Falls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruby_Falls), and the [Tennessee Aquarium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee_Aquarium) in Chattanooga; the [American Museum of Science and Energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Museum_of_Science_and_Energy) in Oak Ridge, the [Bristol Motor Speedway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bristol_Motor_Speedway), [Jack Daniel's Distillery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jack_Daniel's_Distillery) in Lynchburg, and the [Hiwassee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hiwassee_River) and [Ocoee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toccoa/Ocoee_River) rivers in Polk County.[^cite_note-traveltips-361]

The [National Park Service](161535.md) preserves four Civil War battlefields in Tennessee: [Chickamauga and Chattanooga National Military Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chickamauga_and_Chattanooga_National_Military_Park), [Stones River National Battlefield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stones_River_National_Battlefield), [Shiloh National Military Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shiloh_National_Military_Park), and [Fort Donelson National Battlefield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Donelson_National_Battlefield).[^cite_note-364] The NPS also operates [Big South Fork National River and Recreation Area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_South_Fork_National_River_and_Recreation_Area), [Cumberland Gap National Historical Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cumberland_Gap_National_Historical_Park), [Overmountain Victory National Historic Trail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Overmountain_Victory_National_Historic_Trail), [Trail of Tears National Historic Trail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trail_of_Tears#Landmarks_and_commemorations), [Andrew Johnson National Historic Site](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrew_Johnson_National_Historic_Site), and the [Manhattan Project National Historical Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manhattan_Project_National_Historical_Park).[^cite_note-365] Tennessee is home to eight [National Scenic Byways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Scenic_Byways), including the [Natchez Trace Parkway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natchez_Trace_Parkway), the [East Tennessee Crossing Byway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Tennessee_Crossing_Byway), the [Great River Road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_River_Road), the [Norris Freeway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Route_441_in_Tennessee), [Cumberland National Scenic Byway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee_State_Route_63), [Sequatchie Valley Scenic Byway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee_State_Route_111), [The Trace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Trace_\(Land_Between_the_Lakes\)), and the [Cherohala Skyway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cherohala_Skyway).[^cite_note-scenicbyway-366][^cite_note-fivenewroads-367] Tennessee maintains 65 [state parks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Tennessee_state_parks_and_natural_areas), covering 132,000 acres (530 km<sup>2</sup>).[^cite_note-368] Many reservoirs created by TVA dams have also generated water-based tourist attractions.[^cite_note-flessner-369]

## Culture

Further information: [Literature of Tennessee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Literature_of_Tennessee)



[Dolly Parton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dolly_Parton), pictured in 1977, is known as the "Queen of Country" and is considered a personification of Tennessee, particularly the mountainous region of East Tennessee.

Tennessee blends southern [Appalachian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Appalachia#Culture) and [Southern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_the_Southern_United_States) flavors, which originate from its English, Scotch-Irish, and African roots, and has evolved as it has grown. Its [Grand Divisions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Divisions) also manifest into distinct cultural regions, with East Tennessee commonly associated with Southern Appalachia,[^cite_note-370] and Middle and West Tennessee commonly associated with [Upland Southern culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Upland_South#History_and_culture) and sometimes even [Deep Southern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deep_South) culture.[^cite_note-371] Parts of West Tennessee, especially Memphis, are sometimes considered part of the Deep South.[^cite_note-372] The [Tennessee State Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee_State_Museum) in Nashville chronicles the state's history and culture.[^cite_note-373]

Tennessee is perhaps best known culturally for its musical heritage and contributions to the development of many forms of [popular music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Popular_music), particularly in the country genre.[^cite_note-huffpost-374][^cite_note-entertainment.howstuffworks.com-375] Notable authors with ties to Tennessee include [Cormac McCarthy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cormac_McCarthy), [Peter Taylor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_Taylor_\(writer\)), [James Agee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Agee), [Frances Hodgson Burnett](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frances_Hodgson_Burnett), [Thomas S. Stribling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_S._Stribling), [Ida B. Wells](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ida_B._Wells), [Nikki Giovanni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nikki_Giovanni), [Shelby Foote](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shelby_Foote), [Ann Patchett](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ann_Patchett), [Ishmael Reed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ishmael_Reed), and [Randall Jarrell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Randall_Jarrell). The state's well-known contributions to [Southern cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuisine_of_the_Southern_United_States) include [Memphis-style barbecue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Memphis-style_barbecue), [Nashville hot chicken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hot_chicken), and [Tennessee whiskey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee_whiskey).[^cite_note-376]

### Music

Main articles: [Music of Tennessee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_Tennessee) and [Music of East Tennessee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_East_Tennessee)

A performance of the Grand Ole Opry in Nashville

The *[Grand Ole Opry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Ole_Opry)*, which was recorded in Nashville's [Ryman Auditorium](1034005.md) from 1943 to 1974, is the longest-running [radio broadcast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_broadcast) in US history.

[^cite_note-entertainment.howstuffworks.com-375]

Tennessee has played a critical role in the development of many forms of American popular music, including [blues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blues), [country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Country_music), [rock and roll](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rock_and_roll), [rockabilly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rockabilly), [soul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soul_music), [bluegrass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bluegrass_music), [Contemporary Christian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contemporary_Christian_music), and [gospel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gospel_music). Many consider Memphis's [Beale Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beale_Street) the epicenter of the blues, with musicians such as [W. C. Handy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/W._C._Handy) performing in its clubs as early as 1909.[^cite_note-huffpost-374] Memphis was historically home to [Sun Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sun_Records), where musicians such as [Elvis Presley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elvis_Presley), [Johnny Cash](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Johnny_Cash), [Carl Perkins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carl_Perkins), [Jerry Lee Lewis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jerry_Lee_Lewis), [Roy Orbison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roy_Orbison), and [Charlie Rich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charlie_Rich) began their recording careers, and where rock and roll took shape in the 1950s.[^cite_note-huffpost-374] [Stax Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stax_Records) in Memphis became one of the most important labels for soul artists in the late 1950s and 1960s, and a subgenre known as [Memphis soul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Memphis_soul) emerged.[^cite_note-377] The [1927 Victor recording sessions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bristol_sessions) in [Bristol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bristol,_Tennessee) generally mark the beginning of the country music genre and the rise of the *[Grand Ole Opry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Ole_Opry)* in the 1930s helped make Nashville the center of the country music recording industry.[^cite_note-378][^cite_note-379] [Dolly Parton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dolly_Parton), known as the "Queen of Country", was from near [Sevierville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sevierville,_Tennessee) and is considered the best selling female country artist of all time.[^cite_note-380] Considered a cultural icon and personification of the state, Parton is also known internationally for her music, personality, filmography, business ventures, philanthropic work, and support of the [Great Smoky Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Smoky_Mountains).[^cite_note-381][^cite_note-382] Nashville became known as "Music City", and the *Grand Ole Opry* remains the nation's longest-running radio show.[^cite_note-entertainment.howstuffworks.com-375]

Many museums and historic sites recognize Tennessee's role in nurturing various forms of popular music, including [Sun Studio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sun_Studio), [Memphis Rock N' Soul Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Memphis_Rock_N'_Soul_Museum), [Stax Museum of American Soul Music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stax_Museum_of_American_Soul_Music), and [Blues Hall of Fame](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blues_Hall_of_Fame) in Memphis, the [Ryman Auditorium](1034005.md), [Country Music Hall of Fame and Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Country_Music_Hall_of_Fame_and_Museum), [Musicians Hall of Fame and Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Musicians_Hall_of_Fame_and_Museum), [National Museum of African American Music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Museum_of_African_American_Music), and [Music Row](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_Row) in Nashville, the International Rock-A-Billy Museum in Jackson, the [Mountain Music Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mountain_Music_Museum) in Kingsport, and the [Birthplace of Country Music Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Birthplace_of_Country_Music_Museum) in Bristol.[^cite_note-383] The [Rockabilly Hall of Fame](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rockabilly_Hall_of_Fame), an online site recognizing the development of rockabilly, is also based in Nashville. Several annual music festivals take place throughout the state, the largest of which are the [Beale Street Music Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Memphis_in_May#Beale_Street_Music_Festival_\(BSMF\)) in Memphis, the [CMA Music Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CMA_Music_Festival) in Nashville, [Bonnaroo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bonnaroo_Music_Festival) in [Manchester](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manchester,_Tennessee), and [Riverbend](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riverbend_Festival) in Chattanooga.[^cite_note-384]

## Education

See also: [List of school districts in Tennessee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_school_districts_in_Tennessee) and [List of high schools in Tennessee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_high_schools_in_Tennessee)

Education in Tennessee is administered by the Tennessee Department of Education.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELyons,_Scheb,_&_Stair2001286–287-385] The state Board of Education has 11 members: one from each Congressional district, a student member, and the executive director of the [Tennessee Higher Education Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee_Higher_Education_Commission) (THEC), who serves as ex-officio nonvoting member.[^cite_note-386] Public primary and secondary education systems are operated by county, city, or special school districts to provide education at the local level, and operate under the direction of the Tennessee Department of Education.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELyons,_Scheb,_&_Stair2001286–287-385] The state also has many private schools.[^cite_note-387]

The state enrolls approximately 1 million [K–12](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K%E2%80%9312) students in 137 districts.[^cite_note-edchoices-388] In 2025, the four-year high school graduation rate was 92.3%, a record high.[^cite_note-389] That year, Tennessee spent \$12,147 per student, the lowest in the nation.[^cite_note-390] Tennessee implemented a statewide [school voucher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/School_voucher) program for the 2025–26 school year, despite strong opposition.[^cite_note-391][^cite_note-392] An expansion of the program is set to take effect the following academic year.[^cite_note-393][^cite_note-394]

### Colleges and universities

Main article: [List of colleges and universities in Tennessee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_colleges_and_universities_in_Tennessee)

Kirkland Hall at Vanderbilt University in Nashville

[Vanderbilt University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vanderbilt_University) in Nashville is consistently ranked as one of the top research institutions in the nation

Public higher education is overseen by the [Tennessee Higher Education Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee_Higher_Education_Commission) (THEC), which provides guidance to the state's two public university systems. The [University of Tennessee system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Tennessee_system) operates four primary campuses in [Knoxville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Tennessee), [Chattanooga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Tennessee_at_Chattanooga), [Martin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Tennessee_at_Martin), and [Pulaski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Tennessee_Southern); a [Health Sciences Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Tennessee_Health_Science_Center) in Memphis; and an [aerospace research facility](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Tennessee_Space_Institute) in Tullahoma.[^cite_note-395] The [Tennessee Board of Regents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee_Board_of_Regents) (TBR), also known as The College System of Tennessee, operates 13 community colleges and 27 campuses of the [Tennessee Colleges of Applied Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee_Colleges_of_Applied_Technology) (TCAT).[^cite_note-396] Until 2017, the TBR also operated six public universities in the state; it now only gives them administrative support.[^cite_note-397]

In 2014, the Tennessee General Assembly created the [Tennessee Promise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee_Promise), which allows in-state high school graduates to enroll in two-year post-secondary education programs such as associate degrees and certificates at community colleges and [trade schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trade_school) in Tennessee tuition-free, funded by the state lottery, if they meet certain requirements.[^cite_note-tnpromise-398] The Tennessee Promise was created as part of then-governor [Bill Haslam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_Haslam)'s "Drive to 55" program, which set a goal of increasing the number of college-educated residents to at least 55% of the state's population.[^cite_note-tnpromise-398] The program has also received national attention, with multiple states having since created similar programs modeled on the Tennessee Promise.[^cite_note-399]

Tennessee has 103 private institutions.[^cite_note-400] [Vanderbilt University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vanderbilt_University) in Nashville is consistently ranked as one of the nation's leading research institutions.[^cite_note-401] Nashville is often called the "Athens of the South" due to its many colleges and universities.[^cite_note-Kreyling1996-402] Tennessee is also home to six [historically Black colleges and universities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Historically_Black_colleges_and_universities) (HBCUs).[^cite_note-403]

## Media

See also: [List of television stations in Tennessee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_television_stations_in_Tennessee), [List of newspapers in Tennessee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_newspapers_in_Tennessee), and [List of radio stations in Tennessee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_radio_stations_in_Tennessee)

Offices of The Tennessean, a newspaper in Nashville

Offices of *[The Tennessean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Tennessean)* in Nashville

Tennessee is home to more than 120 newspapers. The most-circulated paid newspapers in the state include *[The Tennessean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Tennessean)* in Nashville, *[The Commercial Appeal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Commercial_Appeal)* in Memphis, the *[Knoxville News Sentinel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Knoxville_News_Sentinel)*, the *[Chattanooga Times Free Press](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chattanooga_Times_Free_Press)*, and *[The Leaf-Chronicle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Leaf-Chronicle)* in Clarksville, *[The Jackson Sun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Jackson_Sun)*, and *[The Daily News Journal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Daily_News_Journal)* in Murfreesboro. All of these except the *Times Free Press* are owned by [Gannett](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gannett).[^cite_note-404]

Six [television media markets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_United_States_television_markets)—Nashville, Memphis, Knoxville, Chattanooga, Tri-Cities, and Jackson—are based in Tennessee. The Nashville market is the third-largest in the [Upland South](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Upland_South) and the ninth-largest in the southeastern United States, according to [Nielsen Media Research](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nielsen_Media_Research). Small sections of the Huntsville, Alabama and [Paducah, Kentucky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paducah,_Kentucky)-[Cape Girardeau, Missouri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Girardeau,_Missouri)-[Harrisburg, Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harrisburg,_Illinois) markets also extend into the state.[^cite_note-405] Tennessee has 43 full-power and 41 [low-power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Low-power_broadcasting) television stations[^cite_note-406] and more than 450 [Federal Communications Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Communications_Commission) (FCC)-licensed radio stations. The *[Grand Ole Opry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Ole_Opry)*, based in Nashville, is the longest-running radio show in the country, having broadcast continuously since 1925.[^cite_note-entertainment.howstuffworks.com-375]

## Transportation

The [Tennessee Department of Transportation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee_Department_of_Transportation) (TDOT) is the primary agency that is tasked with regulating and maintaining Tennessee's transportation infrastructure.[^cite_note-407] Tennessee is currently one of five states with no transportation-related debts.[^cite_note-408][^cite_note-409] TDOT's long-standing tradition of not incurring debt to fund transportation has been lauded by supporters who argue that it maintains fiscal responsibility. It is also been criticized by some who claim it hinders the state's ability to address all necessary infrastructural needs.[^cite_note-TACIR-410][^cite_note-411]

### Roads

Further information: [List of Interstate Highways in Tennessee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Interstate_Highways_in_Tennessee), [List of U.S. Highways in Tennessee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._Highways_in_Tennessee), [List of state routes in Tennessee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_state_routes_in_Tennessee), and [Tennessee State Route System](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee_State_Route_System)

Photograph of Interstate 40 in Nashville. Interstate 40 traverses Tennessee from east to west, and serve's the state's three largest cities.

[Interstate 40](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_40_in_Tennessee) traverses Tennessee from east to west, and serves the state's three largest cities.

Tennessee has 96,187 miles (154,798 km) of roads, of which 14,463 miles (23,276 km) are maintained by the state.[^cite_note-transoverview-412] Of the state's highways, 1,201 miles (1,933 km) are part of the [Interstate Highway System](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_Highway_System). Tennessee has no [tolled](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toll_roads_in_the_United_States) roads or bridges but has the sixth-highest mileage of [high-occupancy vehicle (HOV) lanes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High-occupancy_vehicle_lane), which are utilized on freeways in the congestion-prone Nashville and Memphis metropolitan areas.[^cite_note-413] In 2023, [express toll lanes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Express_toll_lanes) called [Choice Lanes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Choice_Lanes) were authorized in Tennessee.[^cite_note-414] Owing to its central location and heavy truck traffic, the state consistently has some of the highest numbers of [traffic congestion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traffic_congestion) bottlenecks in the country.[^cite_note-TACIR-410][^cite_note-415] Tennessee also consistently ranks poorly for [road safety](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transportation_safety_in_the_United_States), with one of the highest [traffic fatality rates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._states_by_road_deaths) in the country.[^cite_note-TACIR-410]

[Interstate 40](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_40_in_Tennessee) (I-40) is the longest Interstate Highway in Tennessee, traversing the state for 455 miles (732 km).[^cite_note-fhwa-416] I-40 serves the major cities of Memphis, Nashville, and Knoxville, and throughout its entire length in Tennessee, one can observe the diversity of the state's geography and landforms.[^cite_note-maertens-206][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMoore1994xxiii–xl-417] I-40's branch Interstates include [I-240](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_240_\(Tennessee\)) in Memphis; [I-440](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_440_\(Tennessee\)) in Nashville; [I-840](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_840_\(Tennessee\)) around Nashville; [I-140](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pellissippi_Parkway) from Knoxville to [Maryville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maryville,_Tennessee); and [I-640](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/I-640) in Knoxville. In a north–south orientation, from west to east, are Interstates [55](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_55_in_Tennessee), which serves Memphis; [65](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_65_in_Tennessee), which passes through Nashville; [75](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_75_in_Tennessee), which serves Chattanooga and Knoxville; and [81](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_81_in_Tennessee), which begins east of Knoxville, and serves Bristol to the northeast. [I-24](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_24_in_Tennessee) is an east–west Interstate that enters the state in Clarksville, passes through Nashville, and terminates in Chattanooga. [I-26](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Route_23_in_Tennessee), although technically an east–west Interstate, begins in Kingsport and runs southwardly through [Johnson City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Johnson_City,_Tennessee) before exiting into North Carolina. [I-155](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_155_\(Missouri-Tennessee\)) is a branch route of I-55 that serves the northwestern part of the state. [I-275](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_275_\(Tennessee\)) is a short spur route in Knoxville. [I-269](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_269) runs from [Millington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Millington,_Tennessee) to [Collierville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collierville,_Tennessee), serving as an outer bypass of Memphis.[^cite_note-fhwa-416][^cite_note-tdotmap-418]

### Airports

Main article: [List of airports in Tennessee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_airports_in_Tennessee)

Photograph of Memphis International Airport, showing a row of FedEx planes

[Memphis International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Memphis_International_Airport), the hub of [FedEx Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FedEx), often ranks as the busiest cargo airport in the world

Major airports in Tennessee include [Nashville International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nashville_International_Airport) (BNA), [Memphis International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Memphis_International_Airport) (MEM), [McGhee Tyson Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McGhee_Tyson_Airport) (TYS) outside of Knoxville, [Chattanooga Metropolitan Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chattanooga_Metropolitan_Airport) (CHA), [Tri-Cities Regional Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tri-Cities_Regional_Airport) (TRI) in [Blountville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blountville,_Tennessee), and [McKellar-Sipes Regional Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McKellar-Sipes_Regional_Airport) (MKL) in Jackson. Because Memphis International Airport is the hub of [FedEx Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FedEx_Corporation), it sometimes ranks as the [world's second-busiest cargo airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_busiest_airports_by_cargo_traffic).[^cite_note-419] The state also has 70 [general aviation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_aviation) airports and 148 [heliports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heliport).[^cite_note-transoverview-412]

### Railroads

Main article: [List of Tennessee railroads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Tennessee_railroads)

For passenger rail service, Memphis and [Newbern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newbern,_Tennessee) are served by the [Amtrak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amtrak) [City of New Orleans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_of_New_Orleans_\(train\)) line on its run between [Chicago](6886.md) and [New Orleans](53842.md).[^cite_note-420] Nashville is served by the [WeGo Star](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WeGo_Star) [commuter rail](6286.md) service.[^cite_note-421] Tennessee currently has 2,604 miles (4,191 km) of freight trackage in operation,[^cite_note-422] most of which are owned by [CSX Transportation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CSX_Transportation).[^cite_note-423] [Norfolk Southern Railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norfolk_Southern_Railway) also operates lines in East and southwestern Tennessee.[^cite_note-424] [BNSF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BNSF) operates a major [intermodal facility](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_hub) in Memphis.

### Waterways

Tennessee has a total of 976 miles (1,571 km) of [navigable waterways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inland_waterways_of_the_United_States), the 11th highest in the nation.[^cite_note-transoverview-412] These include the [Mississippi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mississippi_River), [Tennessee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee_River), and [Cumberland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cumberland_River) rivers.[^cite_note-425] Five inland ports are located in the state, including the [Port of Memphis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Memphis), which is the fifth largest in the United States and the second largest on the Mississippi River.[^cite_note-426]

## Law and government

Main article: [Government of Tennessee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Tennessee)

Photograph of the Tennessee State Capitol in Nashville

The [Tennessee State Capitol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee_State_Capitol) in [Nashville](22018.md) is one of the oldest working statehouses in the country, having been used continuously since 1853

The [Constitution of Tennessee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Tennessee) was adopted in 1870. The state had two previous constitutions. The first was adopted in 1796, the year Tennessee was admitted to the union, and the second in 1834. Since 1826, [Nashville](22018.md) has been the capital of Tennessee. The capital was previously in three other cities.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELangsdon2000104-427] [Knoxville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Knoxville,_Tennessee) was the capital from statehood in 1796 until 1812,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELangsdon2000104-427] except for September 21, 1807, when the legislature met in [Kingston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingston,_Tennessee) for a day.[^cite_note-428] The capital was relocated to Nashville in 1812, where it remained until it was relocated back to Knoxville in 1817. The next year, the capital was moved to [Murfreesboro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Murfreesboro,_Tennessee), where it remained until 1826. Nashville was officially named Tennessee's permanent capital in 1843.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELangsdon2000104-427] The [state capitol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee_State_Capitol) building was constructed between 1845 and 1859, It has been used by the legislature and executive branch continuously since 1853, making it one of the oldest working statehouses in the U.S.[^cite_note-429]

### Executive and legislative branches

See also: [List of governors of Tennessee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_governors_of_Tennessee)

Like the federal government, Tennessee's government has three branches. The executive branch is led by the [governor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governor_of_Tennessee), who holds office for a four-year term and may serve a maximum of two consecutive terms.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELyons,_Scheb,_&_Stair200148–49-430] The governor is the only official elected statewide. The current governor is [Bill Lee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_Lee_\(Tennessee_politician\)), a [Republican](32070.md). The governor is supported by 22 cabinet-level departments, most headed by a commissioner the governor appoints. The executive branch also includes several agencies, boards, and commissions, some of which are under the auspices of one of the cabinet-level departments.[^cite_note-431]

The [bicameral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bicameralism) legislative branch, the [Tennessee General Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee_General_Assembly), consists of the 33-member [Senate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee_Senate) and the 99-member [House of Representatives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee_House_of_Representatives).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELyons,_Scheb,_&_Stair2001232–233-432] Senators serve four-year terms and House members serve two-year terms.[^cite_note-capitolabout-433] Each chamber chooses a Speaker, who is elected by a joint session of the legislature.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELyons,_Scheb,_&_Stair2001190–191-434] The Speaker of the Senate also serves as the [lieutenant governor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lieutenant_Governor_of_Tennessee), a practice found only in one other state, and the House Speaker is third in line for the governorship.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELyons,_Scheb,_&_Stair200148–49-430] The legislature can override a [veto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Veto_power_in_the_United_States) by a simple majority, and the state has no "[pocket veto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pocket_veto)".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELyons,_Scheb,_&_Stair200148–49-430] The legislature convenes at noon on the second Tuesday in January and meets for a total of 90 days over two sessions, usually adjourning in late April or early May.[^cite_note-capitolabout-433] Special sessions may be called by the governor or by two-thirds of the members of both chambers.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELyons,_Scheb,_&_Stair200172-435]

### Judicial system

See also: [Courts of Tennessee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Courts_of_Tennessee)

Tennessee's highest court is the [Tennessee Supreme Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee_Supreme_Court).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELyons,_Scheb,_&_Stair200151–52-436] It has a [chief justice and four associate justices](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_justices_of_the_Tennessee_Supreme_Court).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELyons,_Scheb,_&_Stair200151–52-436] No more than two justices can be from the same [Grand Division](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Divisions_of_Tennessee).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELyons,_Scheb,_&_Stair200151–52-436] The Supreme Court of Tennessee appoints the state's [Attorney General](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee_Attorney_General), a practice only found in Tennessee.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELyons,_Scheb,_&_Stair2001132-437] Both the [Court of Appeals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee_Court_of_Appeals) and the [Court of Criminal Appeals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee_Court_of_Criminal_Appeals) have 12 judges, who are evenly from each Grand Division.[^cite_note-438] Under the [Tennessee Plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee_Plan), the governor appoints justices on all three courts to eight-year terms; they must be [retained](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Retention_election) by the voters during the first general election after appointment and at the end of their term.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELyons,_Scheb,_&_Stair2001167–168-439] Tennessee is divided into 31 judicial districts, each with a circuit and chancery court, and a [district attorney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/District_attorney) and judges elected to eight-year terms. Separate criminal courts serve 13 of the 31 judicial districts; circuit courts handle criminal cases in the remaining districts. Local courts include general sessions, juvenile and domestic, and municipal courts.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELyons,_Scheb,_&_Stair2001159–161-440]

Tennessee maintains four dedicated law enforcement agencies: the [Tennessee Highway Patrol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee_Highway_Patrol) (THP), the [Tennessee Wildlife Resources Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee_Wildlife_Resources_Agency) (TWRA), the [Tennessee Bureau of Investigation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee_Bureau_of_Investigation) (TBI), and the [Tennessee Department of Environment and Conservation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee_Department_of_Environment_and_Conservation) (TDEC). The Highway Patrol is the primary entity that enforces highway safety regulations and general non-[wildlife](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wildlife) state laws. It is under the jurisdiction of the Department of Safety. The TWRA is an independent agency tasked with enforcing all wildlife, boating, and fishery regulations outside of state parks. TDEC enforces state environmental laws and regulations. The TBI is the primary state-level criminal investigative department. State [park rangers](499846.md) are responsible for all activities and law enforcement inside the Tennessee State Parks system. Tennessee, like many other southern states, has a strong reputation for harsh criminal punishment. [Capital punishment](47138096.md) is legal in Tennessee and has existed at various times since statehood.[^cite_note-StateExecution-441][^cite_note-442] [Lethal injection](62745.md) is the primary means of execution, but [electrocution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electrocution) is also allowed.[^cite_note-time20140522-443][^cite_note-reuters20140523-444] In 2026, Tennessee is set to execute [Christa Gail Pike](15838471.md), the first female to be executed in over 200 years.[^cite_note-445]

### Local

Tennessee is divided into [95 counties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_counties_in_Tennessee), with 92 county governments that use a [county commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/County_commission) legislative body and a separately elected county executive. The governments of [Davidson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Davidson_County,_Tennessee) (Nashville), [Moore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moore_County,_Tennessee) ([Lynchburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lynchburg,_Tennessee)), and [Trousdale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trousdale_County,_Tennessee) ([Hartsville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hartsville,_Tennessee)) are [consolidated with their county seats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Consolidated_city-county). Each county elects a sheriff, property assessor, trustee, register of deeds, and county clerk.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELyons,_Scheb,_&_Stair200152–53-446] Tennessee has more than 340 municipalities. Most cities and towns use the [weak mayor-council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mayor%E2%80%93council_government#Weak-mayor_government_form) (mayor-aldermen), [strong-mayor council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mayor%E2%80%93council_government#Strong-mayor_government_form), [city commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_commission_government), or [council–manager](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council%E2%80%93manager_government) forms of government. Local law enforcement is divided between county sheriff's offices and municipal police departments. In every county except Davidson, the sheriff is the chief law enforcement officer.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELyons,_Scheb,_&_Stair2001385-447]

### Federal

See also: [Tennessee's congressional delegations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee's_congressional_delegations) and [Tennessee's congressional districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee's_congressional_districts)

Tennessee sends [nine representatives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee's_congressional_districts#Current_districts_and_representatives) to the [United States House of Representatives](19468510.md). The current delegation consists of eight [Republicans](32070.md) and one [Democrat](5043544.md). Its [U.S. senators](24909346.md) are [Marsha Blackburn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marsha_Blackburn) and [Bill Hagerty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_Hagerty), both Republicans. Tennessee is under the jurisdiction of the [Sixth Circuit Court of Appeals](428328.md), which has jurisdiction over three [district courts in the state](70235.md): the [Eastern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_District_Court_for_the_Eastern_District_of_Tennessee), [Middle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_District_Court_for_the_Middle_District_of_Tennessee), and [Western](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_District_Court_for_the_Western_District_of_Tennessee) districts.[^cite_note-448]

### Tribal

The [Mississippi Band of Choctaw Indians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mississippi_Band_of_Choctaw_Indians) is Tennessee's only federally recognized [Native American tribe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tribe_\(Native_American\)). It owns 79 acres (32 ha) in [Henning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henning,_Tennessee), which the tribe placed into federal trust in 2012. This is governed directly by the tribe.[^cite_note-449]

## Politics

See also: [Political party strength in Tennessee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_party_strength_in_Tennessee)

The politics of Tennessee is dominated by the [Republican Party](32070.md).[^cite_note-polarization-450][^cite_note-knoxnews1120-451] Republicans currently hold both of the state's [U.S. Senate seats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_United_States_senators_from_Tennessee), eight out of nine [Congressional seats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee's_congressional_districts), 75 of 99 state [House seats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee_House_of_Representatives), and 27 of 33 [state Senate seats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee_Senate). Democratic strength is largely concentrated in [Nashville](22018.md), [Memphis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Memphis,_Tennessee), and in the urban centers of [Knoxville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Knoxville,_Tennessee), [Chattanooga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chattanooga,_Tennessee), and [Clarksville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clarksville,_Tennessee). Several suburban areas of Nashville and Memphis also contain significant Democratic minorities. Tennessee is one of thirteen states which holds its presidential primaries on [Super Tuesday](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Tuesday).[^cite_note-ST2020-452]

### Voting rights and electoral law

Tennessee does not require voters to [declare a party affiliation when registering](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open_primaries_in_the_United_States). The state is one of ten that require voters to present a [photo identification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voter_identification_laws_in_the_United_States) as of 2026.[^cite_note-453][^cite_note-454] Tennessee has one of the highest rates of [felony disenfranchisement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Felony_disenfranchisement_in_the_United_States) in the U.S., with nearly 10% of its voting-age population ineligible to vote. The state also has the highest rate of Black and Latino felony disenfranchisement.[^cite_note-455][^cite_note-456] Tennessee consistently has one of the lowest rates of voter registration and participation.[^cite_note-457][^cite_note-458]

Tennessee has a long history of contested redistricting and [gerrymandering](12987.md). In the landmark *[Baker v. Carr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baker_v._Carr)* case, the [U.S. Supreme Court](31737.md) ruled in 1962 that courts could review state legislative districting after Tennessee had not redrawn its districts from 1901 to 1961. This left rural areas with much more political power than growing cities like Nashville and Memphis.[^cite_note-459][^cite_note-460] More recently, Tennessee's [2022 congressional and state maps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_United_States_House_of_Representatives_elections_in_Tennessee#Redistricting) were challenged because they split [Davidson County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Davidson_County,_Tennessee) and other parts of the Nashville metropolitan area in ways critics said reduced the influence of urban and minority voters. Federal courts dismissed the racial gerrymandering claims, and the Tennessee Supreme Court later approved the maps for state legislative districts.[^cite_note-461] In response to the *[Louisiana v. Callais](80600781.md)* U.S. Supreme Court case, Tennessee legislators [redrew the state's districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Tennessee_redistricting) in 2026 to split majority-black Memphis into three districts.[^cite_note-462] Memphis had previously been placed in a single district to comply with the [Voting Rights Act of 1965](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voting_Rights_Act_of_1965), and critics decried this split as racially motivated.[^cite_note-463][^cite_note-464]

### Historical political alignment



[Al Gore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Gore) served as a U.S. Senator from Tennessee (1985–1993) and as Vice President (1993–2001)

Between the end of the Civil War and the mid-20th century, Tennessee was part of the Democratic [Solid South](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solid_South), but had the largest Republican minority of any former Confederate state.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELangsdon2000x-465] During [Reconstruction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reconstruction_era), freedmen and former free blacks were granted the right to vote; most joined the Republican Party. Numerous African Americans were elected to local offices, and some to state office. However, the Democratic Party regained control of the state in the late 1860s and early 1870s. Following Reconstruction, Tennessee continued to have competitive party politics, but in the 1880s, the White-dominated state government passed [Jim Crow laws](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jim_Crow_laws), including a [poll tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poll_tax) requirement for voter registration, which disenfranchised most African Americans and reduced their political power. After the disenfranchisement of Black voters, the Republican Party became a primarily white sectional party supported mostly in East Tennessee. In the early 1900s, the state legislature approved legislation allowing cities to adopt a commission form of government based on [at-large voting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/At-large_voting) to further limit African American political participation.[^cite_note-466] Not until after passage of the [Voting Rights Act of 1965](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voting_Rights_Act_of_1965) were African Americans able to regain their full voting rights.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELamon198059–60-467]

Portrait of Howard Baker, a United States Senator from Tennessee who became known as "The Great Conciliator"

[Howard Baker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Howard_Baker) served as [Senate Minority and Majority Leader](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Party_leaders_of_the_United_States_Senate) from 1977 to 1985, and was known as "The Great Conciliator"

Between the end of Reconstruction and the mid-20th century, Tennessee voted consistently Democratic in Presidential elections, except in two nationwide Republican landslides in the 1920s. Tennesseans narrowly supported [Warren G. Harding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warren_G._Harding) over Ohio Governor [James Cox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_M._Cox) in 1920,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELangsdon2000299–300-468] and more decisively voted for [Herbert Hoover](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Herbert_Hoover) over New York Governor [Al Smith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Smith) in 1928.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELangsdon2000314-469] During the first half of the 20th century, state politics were dominated by the Democratic [Crump machine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/E._H._Crump#Political_machine) in Memphis.[^cite_note-470] For most of the second half of the 20th century, Tennessee was a [swing state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swing_state) in presidential elections.[^cite_note-polarization2-471] During this time, Democratic presidential nominees from Southern states, including [Lyndon B. Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lyndon_B._Johnson), [Jimmy Carter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jimmy_Carter), and [Bill Clinton](3356.md), tended to fare better in Tennessee than their Northern counterparts, especially among split-ticket voters outside the metropolitan areas. In the 1950s, Tennessee twice voted for Republican [Dwight D.
