Eisenhower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dwight_D._Eisenhower), former Allied Commander of the Armed Forces during World War II.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELangsdon2000350–354-472] [Howard Baker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Howard_Baker), first elected in 1966, became the first Republican U.S. Senator from Tennessee since Reconstruction.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELangsdon2000366–367-473] The Republican [Southern strategy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_strategy) did not have as much of an effect in Tennessee as in most Southern states, but the elections of [Winfield Dunn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Winfield_Dunn) as governor and [Bill Brock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_Brock) to the U.S. Senate in 1970 further helped make the GOP competitive among Whites in statewide elections.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELangsdon2000370–373-474] In the [2000 presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2000_United_States_presidential_election_in_Tennessee), Vice President [Al Gore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Gore), who had previously served as a Democratic U.S. Senator from Tennessee, failed to carry his home state, an unusual occurrence but indicative of strengthening Republican support.[^cite_note-\:12-475]



President [Donald Trump](4848272.md) speaks with [U.S. Senators](24909346.md) [Marsha Blackburn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marsha_Blackburn) and [Bill Hagerty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_Hagerty) after signing a [Presidential Memorandum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidential_memorandum) that will deploy the [National Guard](37988.md) to [Memphis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Memphis,_Tennessee).

Beginning in the early 21st century, Tennessee transitioned into a solid Republican state, primarily due to rural white voters who have rejected the increasing [liberalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modern_liberalism_in_the_United_States) of the Democratic Party.[^cite_note-476][^cite_note-flyer2-477] Republican President [George W. Bush](3414021.md) increased his margin of victory in the state from 4% in 2000 to 14% margin [in 2004](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2004_United_States_presidential_election_in_Tennessee).[^cite_note-478][^cite_note-479] In 2007, [Ron Ramsey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ron_Ramsey) became the first Republican Speaker of the State Senate since Reconstruction,[^cite_note-480] and the following year the Republicans gained control of both houses of the state legislature for the first time since Reconstruction.[^cite_note-481] Voters, however, continued to elect [moderate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moderate_\(politics\)) Republicans, such as centrists [Bill Haslam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_Haslam) and [Lamar Alexander](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lamar_Alexander), until the late 2010s with the rise of [Trumpism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trumpism) in the GOP at a nationwide scale.[^cite_note-plott-482] Since 2016, Tennessee has been the most populous state to vote Republican by more than 60% in presidential elections,[^cite_note-483] and in [2020](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_United_States_presidential_election_in_Tennessee) voted Republican by the largest margin of any state in terms of number of votes.[^cite_note-484] In the 2020s, Tennessee gained national attention for bitter partisan clashes, including disagreements between the legislature and urban areas,[^cite_note-485] and [expulsion of two Democratic legislators](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Tennessee_House_of_Representatives_expulsions) from the state house in 2023.[^cite_note-486]

### State elections

See also: [Elections in Tennessee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elections_in_Tennessee)

Republicans gained full control of the [Tennessee General Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee_General_Assembly) in [2008](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Tennessee_elections) for the first time since Reconstruction, marking a turning point in modern state politics.[^cite_note-487] Since then, the party has expanded its legislative supermajorities, aided by strong support in rural and suburban regions.

The current legislature, the [114th Tennessee General Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/114th_Tennessee_General_Assembly), convened in January 2025 with Republicans holding 27 of 33 seats in the Senate and 75 of 99 in the House. Leadership includes Senate Speaker [Randy McNally](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Randy_McNally) and House Speaker [Cameron Sexton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cameron_Sexton).[^cite_note-488]

In recent election cycles, legislative outcomes have reflected Tennessee's broader partisan shift, with few competitive districts.[^cite_note-489] Elections for state house and senate districts are usually decided in primaries with extremely low voter turnout.[^cite_note-490]

### Federal elections



[2024 U.S. presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_United_States_presidential_election_in_Tennessee) results by county in Tennessee&#x20;

&#x20; Republican

&#x20; Democratic

Tennessee was considered a "[swing state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swing_state)" during the late 20th century, but has voted reliably Republican in presidential elections since [2000](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2000_United_States_presidential_election_in_Tennessee). Republican margins increased significantly in the 2000s and 2010s as rural voters trended toward the GOP.[^cite_note-flyer-491]

[Tennessee's two U.S. senators](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_United_States_senators_from_Tennessee) are in [classes 1 and 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classes_of_United_States_senators). Tennessee has had [nine U.S. House of Representatives seats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee's_congressional_delegations) since the [1980 census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1980_United_States_census), and control of the majority has flipped three times since then ([1994](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1994_United_States_House_of_Representatives_elections_in_Tennessee), [2002](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002_United_States_House_of_Representatives_elections_in_Tennessee), and [2010](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_United_States_House_of_Representatives_elections_in_Tennessee)). Currently, eight representatives are Republican and one is a Democrat.[^cite_note-492]

## Sports









Tennessee's major professional sports franchises. Clockwise from upper left: [Tennessee Titans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee_Titans), [Nashville Predators](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nashville_Predators), [Nashville SC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nashville_SC), and [Memphis Grizzlies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Memphis_Grizzlies).

Tennessee is home to four [major professional sports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Major_professional_sports_leagues_in_the_United_States_and_Canada) franchises:[^cite_note-sportsleaguemaps-493] the [Tennessee Titans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee_Titans) have played in the [National Football League](21211.md) (NFL) since 1997,[^cite_note-PFRTitans-494] the [Nashville Predators](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nashville_Predators) have played in the [National Hockey League](21809.md) (NHL) since 1998,[^cite_note-HRPredators-495] the [Memphis Grizzlies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Memphis_Grizzlies) have played in the [National Basketball Association](22093.md) (NBA) since 2001,[^cite_note-BRGrizzlies-496] and [Nashville SC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nashville_SC) has played in [Major League Soccer](71802.md) (MLS) since 2020.[^cite_note-FBRNashvilleSC-497]

The state is also home to eight minor league teams. Four of these are [Minor League Baseball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minor_League_Baseball) clubs. The [Nashville Sounds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nashville_Sounds), which began play in 1978,[^cite_note-498] and [Memphis Redbirds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Memphis_Redbirds), which began in 1998,[^cite_note-499] each compete in the [International League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_League) at the [Triple-A](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Triple-A_\(baseball\)) level, the highest before [Major League Baseball](38776.md).[^cite_note-500] The [Knoxville Smokies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Knoxville_Smokies), which have played continuously since 1972,[^cite_note-501] and [Chattanooga Lookouts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chattanooga_Lookouts), which have played continuously since 1976,[^cite_note-502] are members of the [Double-A](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Double-A_\(baseball\)) classification [Southern League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_League_\(1964%E2%80%93present\)).[^cite_note-503]

Tennessee has three minor league [soccer](10568.md) teams. [Chattanooga Red Wolves SC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chattanooga_Red_Wolves_SC) has been a member of the third-tier [USL League One](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USL_League_One) since 2019.[^cite_note-504] [One Knoxville SC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/One_Knoxville_SC), founded in 2021, are also members of USL League One since 2023.[^cite_note-505] Founded in 2009, [Chattanooga FC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chattanooga_FC) began playing in the third-tier [MLS Next Pro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MLS_Next_Pro) in 2024.[^cite_note-506] The state also has one minor league [ice hockey](14790.md) team: the [Knoxville Ice Bears](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Knoxville_Ice_Bears), which began play in 2002 and are members of the [Southern Professional Hockey League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Professional_Hockey_League).[^cite_note-507]

The Tennessee Volunteers, the football team of the University of Tennessee

[Tennessee Volunteers football](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee_Volunteers_football)

The state is home to 12 [NCAA Division I](669931.md) programs. Four of these participate in the top level of college football, the [Football Bowl Subdivision](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NCAA_Division_I_Football_Bowl_Subdivision).[^cite_note-NCAA-508] In Knoxville, the [Tennessee Volunteers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee_Volunteers) college teams play in the [Southeastern Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southeastern_Conference) (SEC) of the [National Collegiate Athletic Association](60706.md) (NCAA).[^cite_note-NCAA-508] In Nashville, the [Vanderbilt Commodores](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vanderbilt_Commodores) are also members of the SEC.[^cite_note-NCAA-508] The [Memphis Tigers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Memphis_Tigers) are members of the [American Athletic Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Athletic_Conference), and Murfreesboro's [Middle Tennessee Blue Raiders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_Tennessee_Blue_Raiders) play in [Conference USA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conference_USA).[^cite_note-NCAA-508] Nashville is also home to the [Belmont Bruins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belmont_Bruins), members of the [Ohio Valley Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ohio_Valley_Conference) (OVC) but moving to the [Missouri Valley Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Missouri_Valley_Conference) (MVC) in July 2022; [Tennessee State Tigers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee_State_Tigers_and_Lady_Tigers), OVC members with no plans to change conferences; and the [Lipscomb Bisons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lipscomb_Bisons), members of the [ASUN Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ASUN_Conference).[^cite_note-NCAA-508] Tennessee State plays football in Division I's second level, the [Football Championship Subdivision](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NCAA_Division_I_Football_Championship_Subdivision), while Belmont and Lipscomb [do not have football teams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_NCAA_Division_I_non-football_programs).[^cite_note-NCAA-508] Through the 2021–22 school year, the OVC also includes the [Austin Peay Governors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austin_Peay_Governors) from Clarksville, the [UT Martin Skyhawks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UT_Martin_Skyhawks) from Martin, and the [Tennessee Tech Golden Eagles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee_Tech_Golden_Eagles) from [Cookeville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cookeville,_Tennessee). UT Martin and Tennessee Tech will remain in the OVC, while Peay will move to the ASUN. The [Chattanooga Mocs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chattanooga_Mocs) and Johnson City's [East Tennessee State Buccaneers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Tennessee_State_Buccaneers) are full members, including football, of the [Southern Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Conference).[^cite_note-NCAA-508]

Tennessee is also home to the [Bristol Motor Speedway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bristol_Motor_Speedway), which features [NASCAR Cup Series](56571945.md) racing two weekends a year, routinely selling out more than 160,000 seats on each date.[^cite_note-509] The [Nashville Superspeedway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nashville_Superspeedway) in Lebanon, which previously held [Nationwide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xfinity_Series) and [IndyCar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IndyCar_Series) races until it was shut down in 2011, reopened to host the NASCAR Cup Series in 2021.[^cite_note-510] Tennessee's only graded stakes horserace, the [Iroquois Steeplechase](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iroquois_Steeplechase), is held in Nashville each May.[^cite_note-511] The [WGC Invitational](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WGC_Invitational) is a [PGA Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PGA_Tour) golf tournament that has been held in Memphis since 1958.[^cite_note-512]
